The **2026 AFL season** was the 130th season of the [Australian Football League](4732785.md) (AFL), the highest-level senior men's [Australian rules football](2403.md) competition in Australia. The season featured 18 clubs and ran from 5 March to 26 September, comprising a 23-match home-and-away season over 25 rounds, with a five-week [finals series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL_finals_series) featuring the top ten clubs occurring for the first time.

The [Brisbane Lions](5026.md) won the [premiership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_VFL/AFL_premiers), defeating [Fremantle](312661.md) by seven points in the [2026 AFL Grand Final](80898169.md); it was their third consecutive premiership and sixth AFL premiership overall. Fremantle won the [minor premiership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_VFL/AFL_minor_premiers) by finishing atop the home-and-away ladder with a 19–4 win–loss record. [Collingwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collingwood_Football_Club)'s [Nick Daicos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Daicos) won the [Brownlow Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brownlow_Medal) as the league's best and fairest player and [Sydney](29210.md)'s [Charlie Curnow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Curnow) won his third [Coleman Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleman_Medal) as the league's leading goalkicker.

## Background

In October 2025, the AFL announced two major changes to be brought into the competition for the 2026 season: the [substitute rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interchange_\(Australian_rules_football\)#Substitutes) was removed in favour of a five-player interchange; and the ball would no longer be bounced in the centre of the ground by a field umpire at the start of quarters and after goals, and would be thrown up instead.[^cite_note-bouncesub-1] The removal of the centre bounce ended a tradition dating back to 1887, with AFL executive general manager of football performance [Greg Swann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Swann) explaining that the skill of bouncing the ball was hindering the development of umpires and that the change would allow umpires to focus more on decision-making and reduce the likelihood of players making accidental umpire contact.[^cite_note-bouncesub-1] The AFL announced seven rule changes to reduce game length by approximately three minutes:[^cite_note-2]

- The [6–6–6 rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_Australian_rules_football#0%E2%80%939) would no longer require at least one player from each team to be in the goalsquare for centre ball-ups.

- A 'last disposal' free kick would be awarded if the ball crossed the boundary line from a disposal between the 50-metre arcs, unless a nearby player does not attempt to play the ball or blocks an opponent.

  [^cite_note-3]

- Ruckmen would no longer be allowed to cross the centre line during centre [ball-ups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ball-up), intended to increase the frequency of jumping ruck contests.

- Players taking [kick-ins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kick-in) would only be given eight seconds to restart play, down from approximately twelve seconds.

- Field umpires could immediately restart play at around-the-ground ball-ups if a nominated ruckman was too far away or no nomination was made, however the 'third man up' was still not allowed.

  [^cite_note-4]

- Players who shrug a tackle would be deemed to have had [prior opportunity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_Australian_rules_football#P) when considering [holding the ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holding_the_ball) free kicks.

- Players inside the five-metre protected area after a mark or free kick to an opponent would be required to stand the mark and would no longer be allowed to move out of the area.

Last disposal out of bounds decisions fell within the purview of decisions reviewable by the AFL Review Centre (ARC),[^cite_note-arcreview-5] as had all out of bounds decisions since the 2025 finals series.[^cite_note-6] There was a key error late in the Geelong v Adelaide match in round 3: the boundary umpire incorrectly paid a last disposal free kick to Geelong instead of Adelaide, and Geelong restarted play before ARC had a chance to intervene, ultimately resulting in a goal to Geelong who won by eight points.[^cite_note-7] The AFL admitted the error the following day and announced a review of its ARC review processes.[^cite_note-arcreview-5]

The AFL introduced a wildcard finals round, adding an extra week to the existing finals series and increasing the number of finalists from eight to ten; at the end of the home-and-away season, the seventh-placed team would host the tenth-placed team and eighth would host ninth in wildcard finals, before the remainder of the finals series would continue as per the previous [final eight system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL_finals_systems#Final_eight_system).[^cite_note-wildcard-8] The change was the first change to the AFL finals system since 2000.[^cite_note-9] The change was negatively received by the AFL Fans Association;[^cite_note-wildcard-8] however, sports rights analyst Colin Smith explained that the league was protecting the value of the current \$4.5 billion broadcast right deal by implementing the new system, with more finalists meaning fewer [dead rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_rubber) matches later in the home-and-away season.[^cite_note-10]

A [State of Origin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_matches_in_Australian_rules_football#Australian_rules_State_of_Origin_matches) match took place for the first time since 1999, with [Western Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Australia_Australian_rules_football_team) playing [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Australian_rules_football_team) at [Optus Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perth_Stadium) in February 2026;[^cite_note-11] Victoria won the match by 24 points.[^cite_note-12]

Rules governing voting for the [Brownlow Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brownlow_Medal) were changed, to provide umpires a set of player statistics prior to the lodging of votes.[^cite_note-playerstats-13]

| The crowd of 53,707 for the Fremantle v St Kilda match was the largest crowd for a match between the two clubs.[^cite_note-FREvSTK-64] Fremantle's win over St Kilda was its tenth consecutive win, a club record;[^cite_note-FREvSTK-64] it would win a further four matches to extend the streak to 14 matches.[^cite_note-GWSvFRE-65] North Melbourne trailed by 43 points in the second quarter of its match against Gold Coast and went on to win, recording its biggest comeback since 1947;[^cite_note-66] [Cameron Zurhaar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Zurhaar) (North Melbourne) kicked the winning goal after the final siren.[^cite_note-67] [Scott Pendlebury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Pendlebury) (Collingwood) played his 433rd AFL match against West Coast, breaking the [VFL/AFL games record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VFL/AFL_games_records) held by [Brent Harvey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brent_Harvey) (North Melbourne);[^cite_note-68] the crowd of 90,028 for the match was the largest crowd for a home-and-away match involving a non-Victorian club.[^cite_note-69] Greater Western Sydney scored 14.2 (86) in the third quarter of its match against the Brisbane Lions; this was the highest-scoring quarter in the club's history,[^cite_note-GWSvBL-70] and the highest-scoring quarter against a reigning premier and highest-scoring third quarter in VFL/AFL history.[^cite_note-71] Greater Western Sydney's final score of 26.10 (166) against the Brisbane Lions was its highest ever.[^cite_note-GWSvBL-70] |  |  |  |  |  |

| The crowd of 59,556 for the Hawthorn v Western Bulldogs match was the largest crowd for a home-and-away match between the two clubs.[^cite_note-73] The margin of 124 points in the North Melbourne v Fremantle match was Fremantle's biggest ever win[^cite_note-NMvFRE-74] and the biggest loss of North Melbourne coach [Alastair Clarkson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alastair_Clarkson)'s coaching career.[^cite_note-75] Fremantle kicked the last 19 goals in its win over North Melbourne, the longest unanswered sequence of goals in its history.[^cite_note-NMvFRE-74] The crowd of 21,139 for the Gold Coast v Brisbane Lions match was the largest crowd for a [QClash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QClash) at People First Stadium.[^cite_note-76] The crowd of 88,019 for the Collingwood v Melbourne match was the largest crowd for a home-and-away match between the two clubs since 1958.[^cite_note-77] Six matches in round 13 were decided by margins under ten points, the most in a home-and-away round in VFL/AFL history.[^cite_note-R13-78] |  |  |  |  |  |

| The crowd of 55,966 for the Fremantle v Sydney match was the largest crowd for a home-and-away match between the two clubs[^cite_note-83] and Fremantle's largest home crowd for a home-and-away match outside of a [Western Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Derby).[^cite_note-84] Fremantle's score of 9.5 (59) in the final quarter of its match against Sydney was the highest in a final quarter in its history.[^cite_note-85] Fremantle's final score of 15.21 (111) against Sydney was the highest by a team that went goalless in the first half of a match, having scored 0.11 (11) to half-time.[^cite_note-86] Greater Western Sydney trailed by 39 points in the third quarter of its match against Geelong and went on to win, recording its equal-biggest comeback.[^cite_note-87] The Brisbane Lions' 90-point win was its biggest over Essendon.[^cite_note-88] |  |  |  |  |  |

| The crowd of 14,601 for the Essendon v Greater Western Sydney match was Essendon's smallest home crowd since 2016, excluding matches with restricted crowds during the COVID-19-affected 2020 and 2021 seasons.[^cite_note-89] [Jake Stringer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Stringer) (Greater Western Sydney) kicked seven behinds without a goal in the match against Essendon, the most behinds by a player without kicking a goal since 2003.[^cite_note-90] Essendon's win over Greater Western Sydney ended a 12-match losing streak, and was its first win in Victoria in over a year.[^cite_note-91] |  |  |  |  |  |

| The crowd of 55,010 for the Fremantle v Adelaide match was the largest crowd for a match between the two clubs.[^cite_note-97] Dylan Moore (Hawthorn) had a shot at goal at the final siren to win the match against Collingwood, having [kicked the match-drawing goal against Collingwood earlier in the season](#Round_8), but the ball fell short, resulting in a second draw.[^cite_note-draws-98] The crowd of 24,767 for the Essendon v Sydney match was the smallest for a match involving Essendon at the Melbourne Cricket Ground since 1993, excluding matches with restricted crowds during the [COVID-19-affected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic_on_sports#Australian_rules_football) 2020 and 2021 seasons.[^cite_note-99] Round 23 was the first home-and-away round to feature two draws since 1977.[^cite_note-100] |  |  |  |  |  |

| The crowd of 100,023 for the grand final was the largest crowd for a grand final featuring two non-Victorian clubs.[^cite_note-GF-114] [Shai Bolton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shai_Bolton) (Fremantle) was the first player since 2005 to win the [Norm Smith Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norm_Smith_Medal) in a losing grand final team.[^cite_note-115] The grand final was the most-watched television program of 2026, averaging 6.5 million viewers across the [Seven Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Sport) and [7plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7plus), and the most-watched grand final in VFL/AFL history.[^cite_note-GF-114] |  |  |  |  |  |

## Season notes

- In round 13, the AFL recorded a combined attendance of 390,752, the highest for an eight-match home-and-away round in VFL/AFL history; the round also saw a total television viewership of over 7.2 million, the highest viewership for a home-and-away round in VFL/AFL history despite only eight matches being played.

  [^cite_note-R13-78]

- [Fremantle](312661.md) won 14 consecutive matches during the season,

  [^cite_note-117]

  &#x20;breaking [West Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_Eagles)'s record of 12 in 1991 for the longest winning streak of the Western Australia-based AFL clubs.

  [^cite_note-118]

- Fremantle recorded crowds of over 50,000 in nine consecutive home matches during the home-and-away season, a VFL/AFL record.

  [^cite_note-119]

- [Collingwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collingwood_Football_Club) and [Hawthorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawthorn_Football_Club) drew both of their home-and-away match-ups, becoming the first clubs in VFL/AFL history to do so in the same season;

  [^cite_note-120]

  &#x20;both matches ended with [shots at goal at or after the final siren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_kicks_after_the_siren_in_the_VFL/AFL) by [Dylan Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Moore_\(footballer\)) ([Hawthorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawthorn_Football_Club)) and produced almost identical scorelines: 93–93 in round 8 and 92–92 in round 23.

  [^cite_note-draws-98]

- [Essendon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essendon_Football_Club) won two matches during the home-and-away season, the equal-fewest in the club's history.

  [^cite_note-122]

- New records for combined (7,772,015) and average attendance (37,545) were set during the home-and-away season, along with new records for combined crowds in Western Australia, South Australia and Queensland.

  [^cite_note-123]

## Notable events

Further information: [2026 Sydney Swans player investigation](84013502.md)

- Five [Sydney](29210.md) players, [Riley Bice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riley_Bice), [Nick Blakey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Blakey), [Isaac Heeney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaac_Heeney), [James Jordon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Jordon) and [Chad Warner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chad_Warner), were suspended by the club for the remainder of the season on 19 August 2026, following their involvement in an incident at an [East Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Melbourne) hotel that succeeded the club's 36-point victory over [Essendon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essendon_Football_Club) in round 23. The matter is currently being investigated as a sexual assault by [Victoria Police](1120591.md); as of August 2026, no charges have been laid.

  [^cite_note-124]

## Awards

### Major awards

- The [Coleman Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleman_Medal) was awarded to [Sydney](29210.md)'s [Charlie Curnow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Curnow); he kicked 69 goals to claim his third Coleman Medal, and become the first player since [Lance Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lance_Franklin) in 2014 to win the medal in his first season at a new club.

  [^cite_note-134]

- The [AFL Rising Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL_Rising_Star) was awarded to [Carlton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlton_Football_Club)'s [Jagga Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagga_Smith).

  [^cite_note-135]

- The [Leigh Matthews Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leigh_Matthews_Trophy), awarded to the [AFL Players Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL_Players_Association)'s most valuable player throughout the season, was awarded to [Collingwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collingwood_Football_Club)'s [Nick Daicos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Daicos), for the second successive year.

  [^cite_note-136]

- The [AFL Coaches Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL_Coaches_Association) Champion Player of the Year was awarded to Collingwood's Nick Daicos, who polled 147 votes, the most ever recorded in the award's history.

  [^cite_note-137]
